## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `61254c8d7bb20ecd6088c8617257569f4866415151e84cf9b82761787f6b6460`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-teachers-investigate-an-essay-that-appeared-all-at-once` (Similarity: 0.9280)

```text
A large paste event occurs when a substantial block of text—typically 300 to 1,500+ words—is inserted into an essay document in a single millisecond, indicating that the content was composed in an external application.

In automated writing-process analysis, a large paste event is one of the most prominent visual flags on a document's timeline. While it can represent unauthorized shortcuts (such as pasting an entire essay generated by ChatGPT or copied from an online essay mill), it can also represent legitimate student behavior (such as drafting offline in Microsoft Word or consolidating notes from an outline).

Through Checkmark Plagiarism's Essay Playback , educators can unpack the context surrounding a large paste event, examining active typing duration, downstream revisions, and source matches to determine authentic authorship with accuracy.

Checkmark Plagiarism supports timeline investigations by pairing essay writing playback with AI detection , plagiarism detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: The 4 Potential Meanings of a Large Paste Event
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `a2892849690792e39712c4a8a8bfc63d0de39dd37ddc4adc66aedf178eb425c4`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/a-student-pasted-half-their-essay-does-that-prove-plagiarism` (Similarity: 0.8724)

```text
1. Generative AI Text Insertion

The student prompted ChatGPT, Claude, or Gemini in another window, copied the output, and pasted the entire finished paper into the LMS editor.

2. Direct Plagiarism from Web or Peer

The student copied an article from Wikipedia, Course Hero, or a classmate's Google Doc and pasted it into their assignment.

3. Offline Human Drafting

The student composed the essay legitimately in Microsoft Word, Pages, or Scrivener and transferred the completed draft into the LMS.

4. Consolidating Research Notes & Outlines

The student pasted their own brainstormed bullet points and rough notes into the document before expanding them into full paragraphs.
```

### Section: How Checkmark Essay Playback Analyzes the Paste Event
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `805dfe23bc2b54e5c00026a5684ec268bd1529cd8e81cfbcbb44bcba2423721e`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-students-use-ai-to-hide-plagiarism` (Similarity: 0.9261)

```text
Checkmark Plagiarism's Essay Playback visualizes the entire lifecycle of the document, answering four crucial diagnostic questions:

Total Active Typing Time: Did the student spend 3 hours actively typing in the document before or after the paste, or did the session last only 4 minutes total? 
 Downstream Revision Behavior: Did the student actively edit, restructure, and refine the pasted text, or was it submitted immediately without modification? 
 Raw Paste Payload Content: What exact text was inserted? Does it contain signature AI cliches ( "delve," "tapestry" ) or hallucinated citations? 
 Dual Engine Match Scores: Does the pasted block trigger high AI probability or match direct URLs in the plagiarism database?

Read more in how Checkmark writing process analysis works .
```

### Section: Comparison: Dishonest AI Paste vs. Legitimate Offline Draft Paste
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `4dbbc09d21cf47b3313847f636f788c739b8f59dfa3a4b244ffb5678af6e4183`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-teachers-investigate-an-essay-that-appeared-all-at-once` (Similarity: 0.9140)

```text
Dishonest AI Paste (Integrity Violation)

1,400 words pasted at 11:42 PM; submitted at 11:45 PM. 
 Total active typing duration: 3 minutes. 
 Zero keystroke drafting or paragraph restructuring. 
 Student unable to define vocabulary during oral check-in.

Legitimate Offline Draft Paste (Authentic Writing)

1,000-word draft pasted at 2:00 PM; edited until 5:00 PM. 
 Subsequent active typing: 90 minutes of revisions. 
 Student provides original Word doc with version history. 
 Student fluently explains thesis and sources orally.
```

### Section: A 5-Step Educator Protocol for Large Paste Events
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `791ad67fb84d5c0ee02cb928d7fec07f7e3903d3d60c82f88576483d2b6a6165`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-teachers-tell-when-a-student-pasted-text-into-an-essay` (Similarity: 0.9400)

```text
Educator Large Paste Investigation Checklist:

1. Open the Essay Playback visual replay to evaluate the document timeline. 
 2. Note the timestamp, character volume, and active drafting time before and after the paste. 
 3. Inspect the Original Preserved Paste payload for AI phrasing and fake citations. 
 4. Cross-reference with Checkmark's dual AI and plagiarism detection scores. 
 5. If unauthorized assistance is suspected, invite the student to share offline draft notes in a brief conference.
```

### Section: What is considered a 'large paste event'?
- **Level**: `h3`
- **Hash**: `3230ace533d394aa9bd67560c7cbf9667dad47e375bd1418f8b9abe466e8361f`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-evidence-should-teachers-collect-when-they-suspect-ai-use` (Similarity: 0.8171)

```text
Any single insertion of text exceeding 300 words (or ~1,500 characters) that appears in a document in less than one second.
```

### Section: Does a large paste event automatically mean a student cheated?
- **Level**: `h3`
- **Hash**: `bc8651393c937909964bb8c3b9526d1b349e1be40b67eefa4e96b082dd5d2a87`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-ai-detectors-give-false-positives` (Similarity: 0.8687)

```text
No. It proves the text was composed externally, which could be an authentic draft from Microsoft Word or a paste from ChatGPT. Further investigation is required.
```
